When investors buy more shares than they sell, the funds create new shares and buy bitcoin to back them (an inflow); heavy selling forces share redemptions and bitcoin sales (an outflow). Flows are the cleanest daily read on institutional and retail demand for bitcoin through traditional rails."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"How often is this data updated?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"Twice daily. Issuers disclose creations and redemptions after the US market close; figures for the latest session settle in the evening US time and any late revisions are picked up the following morning. The data was last updated with the Oct 20, 2026 session. The sync last checked for revisions Oct 21, 2026, 6:00 AM ET."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"What was the biggest Bitcoin ETF inflow day ever?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"Nov 7, 2024, when the funds took in a net $1.4B in a single session."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"What was the biggest Bitcoin ETF outflow day ever?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"Feb 25, 2025, when a net $1.1B left the funds in one session."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"Do Bitcoin ETF flows move the price of bitcoin?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"Not one for one. A creation means the fund acquires bitcoin, so steady inflows add buying pressure, but the price is set across the whole market, including exchanges, miners and holders outside the funds. Flows are best read alongside the price: streaks that run against it are where they carry the most information."}},{"@type":"Question","name":"Why do Bitcoin ETF flow numbers change after they are published?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"Issuers report after the US close and some smaller funds report a day late, so the latest session's total can be revised as the remaining numbers arrive. href="/bitcoin-etf-flows/march-2024"><span class="font-serif text-ivory/85">March 2024</span><span class="font-mono text-sm tabular-nums text-ivory/60">+$4.6B</span></a></li><li class="border-b border-rule"><a class="flex items-baseline justify-between gap-3 py-2.5 transition-colors hover:text-bitcoin" href="/bitcoin-etf-flows/february-2024"><span class="font-serif text-ivory/85">February 2024</span><span class="font-mono text-sm tabular-nums text-ivory/60">+$6.0B</span></a></li><li class="border-b border-rule"><a class="flex items-baseline justify-between gap-3 py-2.5 transition-colors hover:text-bitcoin" href="/bitcoin-etf-flows/january-2024"><span class="font-serif text-ivory/85">January 2024</span><span class="font-mono text-sm tabular-nums text-ivory/60">+$1.5B</span></a></li></ul></section><section class="mt-14 max-w-3xl"><h2 class="font-display text-2xl font-semibold text-ivory">How Bitcoin ETF flows work</h2><div class="mt-4 space-y-4 font-serif text-lg leading-relaxed text-ivory/75"><p>A spot Bitcoin ETF holds bitcoin and issues shares against it. When buyers want more shares than exist, a large broker called an authorized participant asks the fund to create new ones, and the fund takes in cash or bitcoin to back them. When holders sell more than buyers want, the process runs in reverse: shares are redeemed and the fund lets bitcoin go. The daily flow is the dollar value of those creations minus the redemptions.</p><p>Flows are different from trading volume. A fund can trade billions of dollars of shares in a day while its flow sits near zero, because most trades are one investor selling to another. Only the change in shares outstanding counts as a flow, which makes flows the cleaner read on new money arriving or leaving.</p><p>Issuers report creations and redemptions after the US market closes. The total for the latest session usually settles that evening, and smaller funds sometimes report a day late, so the most recent figure can move. This page picks those revisions up on the next morning&#x27;s sync.</p></div></section><section class="mt-12 max-w-3xl"><h2 class="font-display text-2xl font-semibold text-ivory">How to read the numbers</h2><div class="mt-4 space-y-4 font-serif text-lg leading-relaxed text-ivory/75"><p>A single day says little on its own. One large allocation, a model-portfolio rebalance or a hedge fund unwinding a basis trade can swing a session by hundreds of millions without saying much about broad demand. Streaks and monthly totals carry more information: weeks of steady inflows mean new buyers are arriving through brokerage accounts, and a run of outflows means existing holders are cashing out.</p><p>Watch where flows and the price disagree. Inflows during a falling price mean someone is buying the dip through the funds; outflows into a rally mean holders are using strength to sell. The cumulative chart above puts the two side by side for that reason.</p><p>Fund-level detail matters too. Much of the early selling came from GBTC, the converted Grayscale trust with a 1.5% fee, as holders moved to cheaper funds. A day of GBTC outflows matched by IBIT inflows is money switching funds while staying in bitcoin. The flows-by-fund table near the top of this page shows which way each issuer moved.</p></div></section><section class="mt-12 max-w-3xl"><h2 class="font-display text-2xl font-semibold text-ivory">What flows leave out</h2><div class="mt-4 space-y-4 font-serif text-lg leading-relaxed text-ivory/75"><p>These numbers cover US spot Bitcoin ETFs only. They leave out bitcoin futures ETFs, funds listed in Canada, Europe and Hong Kong, companies that hold bitcoin on their balance sheets, and everyone who buys coins directly. They also run a day behind the market, since issuers report after the close. Read them as one large slice of demand, measured in public every trading day.</p></div></section><section id="cite" class="mt-14 scroll-mt-24 border border-rule bg-charcoal-2 p-5 sm:p-7"><p class="eyebrow text-bitcoin">Free to use</p><h2 class="mt-1.5 font-display text-2xl font-semibold text-ivory">Cite or embed this data</h2><p class="mt-2 max-w-2xl font-serif text-ivory/70">The numbers are free to quote, chart and republish under CC BY 4.0. All we ask is a credit with a link back to this page.</p><div class="mt-6"><div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-4"><h3 class="font-mono text-xs uppercase tracking-wider text-ivory/55">Credit line</h3><button type="button" class="shrink-0 border border-rule px-2.5 py-1 font-mono text-[11px] text-ivory/70 transition-colors hover:border-bitcoin/60 hover:text-bitcoin">Copy</button></div><p class="mt-2 border border-rule bg-obsidian px-3 py-2.5 font-mono text-[12.5px] leading-relaxed text-ivory/85">Source: The Commoner Bitcoin ETF Tracker by TFTC (tftc.io/bitcoin-etf-flows), data through Oct 20, 2026.</p></div><div class="mt-6"><div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-4"><h3 class="font-mono text-xs uppercase tracking-wider text-ivory/55">Embed the chart (updates itself every day)</h3><button type="button" class="shrink-0 border border-rule px-2.5 py-1 font-mono text-[11px] text-ivory/70 transition-colors hover:border-bitcoin/60 hover:text-bitcoin">Copy HTML</button></div><pre class="mt-2 overflow-x-auto whitespace-pre-wrap break-all border border-rule bg-obsidian px-3 py-2.5 font-mono text-[11.5px] leading-relaxed text-ivory/75">&lt;a href=&quot;https://www.tftc.io/bitcoin-etf-flows&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=&quot;https://www.tftc.io/bitcoin-etf-flows/email-card&quot; width=&quot;600&quot; alt=&quot;Bitcoin ETF flows, the full daily record from TFTC&quot; /&gt;&lt;/a&gt;

Sources, formulas and changes are on the<!-- --> <a href="/bitcoin-etf-flows/methodology" class="text-bitcoin/80 hover:text-bitcoin">methodology page</a>.</p></section><section class="mt-16 max-w-3xl"><h2 class="mb-6 font-display text-3xl font-semibold text-ivory">Frequently Asked Questions</h2><div class="border-t border-dashed border-rule text-left"><details id="faq-what-are-bitcoin-etf-flows-today" class="group scroll-mt-24 border-b border-dashed border-rule"><summary class="flex cursor-pointer list-none items-center justify-between gap-4 py-5 font-display text-xl font-medium text-ivory transition-colors hover:text-bitcoin [&amp;::-webkit-details-marker]:hidden">What are Bitcoin ETF flows today?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-plus h-5 w-5 shrink-0 text-slate transition-transform duration-200 group-open:rotate-45 group-open:text-bitcoin" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M5 12h14"></path><path d="M12 5v14"></path></svg></summary><div class="pb-5 font-serif text-[1.0625rem] leading-relaxed text-ivory/70 [&amp;_p+p]:mt-4 [&amp;_a]:text-bitcoin">The Commoner Bitcoin ETF Tracker from TFTC shows that on Tuesday, Oct 20, 2026, US spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded a net inflow of $23.2M, the 2nd straight trading day of inflows. max-w-3xl\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"h2\",null,{\"className\":\"font-display text-2xl font-semibold text-ivory\",\"children\":\"How Bitcoin ETF flows work\"}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"mt-4 space-y-4 font-serif text-lg leading-relaxed text-ivory/75\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"A spot Bitcoin ETF holds bitcoin and issues shares against it. When buyers want more shares than exist, a large broker called an authorized participant asks the fund to create new ones, and the fund takes in cash or bitcoin to back them. When holders sell more than buyers want, the process runs in reverse: shares are redeemed and the fund lets bitcoin go. The daily flow is the dollar value of those creations minus the redemptions.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"Flows are different from trading volume. A fund can trade billions of dollars of shares in a day while its flow sits near zero, because most trades are one investor selling to another. Only the change in shares outstanding counts as a flow, which makes flows the cleaner read on new money arriving or leaving.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"Issuers report creations and redemptions after the US market closes. The total for the latest session usually settles that evening, and smaller funds sometimes report a day late, so the most recent figure can move. This page picks those revisions up on the next morning's sync.\"}]]}]]}]\n54:[\"$\",\"section\",null,{\"className\":\"mt-12 max-w-3xl\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"h2\",null,{\"className\":\"font-display text-2xl font-semibold text-ivory\",\"children\":\"How to read the numbers\"}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"mt-4 space-y-4 font-serif text-lg leading-relaxed text-ivory/75\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"A single day says little on its own. One large allocation, a model-portfolio rebalance or a hedge fund unwinding a basis trade can swing a session by hundreds of millions without saying much about broad demand. Streaks and monthly totals carry more information: weeks of steady inflows mean new buyers are arriving through brokerage accounts, and a run of outflows means existing holders are cashing out.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"Watch where flows and the price disagree. Inflows during a falling price mean someone is buying the dip through the funds; outflows into a rally mean holders are using strength to sell. The cumulative chart above puts the two side by side for that reason.\"}],[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"Fund-level detail matters too. Much of the early selling came from GBTC, the converted Grayscale trust with a 1.5% fee, as holders moved to cheaper funds. A day of GBTC outflows matched by IBIT inflows is money switching funds while staying in bitcoin. The flows-by-fund table near the top of this page shows which way each issuer moved.\"}]]}]]}]\n55:[\"$\",\"section\",null,{\"className\":\"mt-12 max-w-3xl\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"h2\",null,{\"className\":\"font-display text-2xl font-semibold text-ivory\",\"children\":\"What flows leave out\"}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"mt-4 space-y-4 font-serif text-lg leading-relaxed text-ivory/75\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"These numbers cover US spot Bitcoin ETFs only. They leave out bitcoin futures ETFs, funds listed in Canada, Europe and Hong Kong, companies that hold bitcoin on their balance sheets, and everyone who buys coins directly. They also run a day behind the market, since issuers report after the close.
